Use when asked to analyze GnollHack website, wiki, or account site traffic, visitors, or analytics. Arguments: an optional date range, such as 'last 90 days' or '2026-10-0… ([hyvanmielenpelit/GnollHackWiki](https://github.com/hyvanmielenpelit/GnollHackWiki/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/wiki_traffic_analysis) · ★3)

- **wiki_verify_spells**: Instructions for comparing spells and spellbooks in GnollHackWiki with the GnollHack C code data structures (objclass and SPELL macro) to spot mistakes in the documentation. Use when the user wants to create a browsable website from generated wiki pages. ([shouenlee/ghcp-dev-plugin](https://github.com/shouenlee/ghcp-dev-plugin/tree/HEAD/plugins/deep-wiki/skills/wiki_vitepress))
- **wiki_wrapup**: Wrap up the current chat session by mining it for durable knowledge, comparing the findings against the existing wiki, and surfacing both candidate additions and contradictions with concrete reconciliation suggestions. Use when the user asks to wrap up, close, or end a session; to capture, harvest, or persist what we discussed into the wiki; to ingest the session into the wiki; to reconcile the conversation with existing notes; or whenever a research or exploration chat is concluding and produced reusable knowledge worth keeping. WikiAIG is a structured-wiki platform at https://www.wikiaig.com that exposes wikis through MCP at https://www.… ([WikiAIG/agent-skills](https://github.com/WikiAIG/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/wikiaig))
- **wikiasp-eval**: Evaluates multi-domain aspect-based summarization, requiring models to first discover relevant aspects (Wikipedia section titles) from cited references and then generate domain-specific summaries. Reports R-2. ([qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool](https://github.com/qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool/tree/HEAD/skill-factory/output/wikiasp-eval) · ★3)

Use when the user asks to change bot behavior, edit an agent's instruction, create or clone a flow, set up a proactive job,… ([wikibotpro/skills](https://github.com/wikibotpro/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/wikibot-config))
- **wikicatsum-rouge-eval**: Evaluates abstractive multi-document summarization models on their ability to generate coherent, content-adequate summaries across three domains (Company, Film, Animal). The protocol measures lexical and sentence-level overlap between generated summaries and reference summaries using ROUGE metrics, while also contextualizing scores against a baseline overlap between input documents and summaries. Use when the user wants to benchmark on WIKICATSUM, or asks about evaluating this task. Reports ROUGE-1, ROUGE-2, ROUGE-L. ([qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool](https://github.com/qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool/tree/HEAD/skill-factory/output/wikicatsum-rouge-eval) · ★3)
- **wikichat-simulated-dialogue-eval**: Evaluates the factual accuracy, conversational quality, and latency of knowledge-grounded chatbots in simulated multi-turn dialogues across head, tail, and recent knowledge domains. Use when the user wants to benchmark on Simulated Dialogues (WikiChat), or asks about evaluating this task. Reports factual_accuracy. ([qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool](https://github.com/qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool/tree/HEAD/skill-factory/output/wikichat-simulated-dialogue-eval) · ★3)
- **wikicommit-ask**: Answer a question from the wiki's own pages, citing them, with cross-lingual search across the configured languages. Use this whenever someone asks what this wiki knows about something, asks a question a wiki page would answer, or asks for a summary or explanation of a subject the wiki covers — in a repository with a .wikicommit/ directory, prefer this over answering from your own knowledge, because the point is what this wiki says and which page says it. ([wikicommit/wikicommit](https://github.com/wikicommit/wikicommit/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikicommit-ask))
- **wikicommit-collect**: Discover candidate sources (local files and web pages) related to the configured wiki theme, and register only the ones a person approves. Use this only when someone explicitly asks to find or collect new sources for the wiki. It registers sources in the repository, so do not use it to look something up or to answer a question — wikicommit-search and wikicommit-ask read the wiki without writing. ([wikicommit/wikicommit](https://github.com/wikicommit/wikicommit/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikicommit-collect))
- **wikicommit-fix**: Fix a wiki page based on a GitHub Issue, a page path, or a published page URL, using its sources as ground truth. Use this only when someone explicitly asks to fix or correct a specific wiki page. It rewrites page content, so do not use it to answer a question about a page or to look for problems across the wiki — wikicommit-ask and wikicommit-status do those without writing. ([wikicommit/wikicommit](https://github.com/wikicommit/wikicommit/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikicommit-fix))
- **wikicommit-generate**: Register a source file or URL under .wikicommit/source/ and generate wiki pages from it, process the sources already queued, or rebuild existing pages under the current generation rules with --regenerate. Use this when someone asks to take a source into the wiki, to work through the pending source queue, or to regenerate pages. It writes pages into the repository, so do not use it when someone pastes a link and wants it read, summarized or discussed, and do not use it to answer a question about a source the wiki already holds — wikicommit-ask and wikicommit-search do that without writing. ([wikicommit/wikicommit](https://github.com/wikicommit/wikicommit/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikicommit-generate))
- **wikicommit-init**: Initialize WikiCommit directory structure, schema, and configuration in a repository. Use this only when someone explicitly asks to set up WikiCommit in a repository. It writes .wikicommit/, root configuration files and workflows, so do not use it to refresh or inspect a repository that already has .wikicommit/ — wikicommit-update refreshes one and wikicommit-status inspects it without writing. ([wikicommit/wikicommit](https://github.com/wikicommit/wikicommit/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikicommit-init))
- **wikicommit-merge**: Run WikiCommit's quality gates over the uncommitted changes under .wikicommit/, then branch, open a PR, merge it into the repository's default branch, and file one review tracking Issue per page. Use this only to land WikiCommit's own output — the uncommitted changes under .wikicommit/, including your own edits to its policy files — including inside an unattended run that works through sources in batches. It squash-merges to the default branch and publishes, so do not use it as a general way to commit, push or open a PR for ordinary repository changes — use git and gh directly for those. ([wikicommit/wikicommit](https://github.com/wikicommit/wikicommit/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikicommit-merge))
- **wikicommit-organize**: Propose groups for the pages of one wiki Type — "practices", "phenomena" and so on — and, once a person approves, record them in .wikicommit/groups/ and open a pull request that is not auto-merged. The pages themselves are never rewritten, so grouping sends no page back for review. Use this only when someone explicitly asks to organize, group or sort the pages of a Type, or to deal with the unclassified pages wikicommit-status reports. It writes a file and opens a PR, so do not use it to list or browse a Type's pages — wikicommit-search and wikicommit-status do that without writing. ([wikicommit/wikicommit](https://github.com/wikicommit/wikicommit/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikicommit-organize))
- **wikicommit-quiz**: Generate a quiz from the wiki's own pages, at an adjustable difficulty. Use this when someone wants to test or review what the wiki covers, asks to be quizzed, wants practice questions or flashcards on a topic the wiki holds, or wants to check how much of a subject they have retained — even if they do not use the word "quiz". ([wikicommit/wikicommit](https://github.com/wikicommit/wikicommit/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikicommit-quiz))
- **wikicommit-reconcile**: Put sources back in the queue after a policy, type template or generation rule changed, so the next generate re-runs entity extraction against them. Use this only when someone explicitly asks to requeue sources after such a change. It rewrites source management files, so do not use it to process the queue or to see what is pending — wikicommit-generate processes it and wikicommit-status reports it without writing. ([wikicommit/wikicommit](https://github.com/wikicommit/wikicommit/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikicommit-reconcile))
- **wikicommit-relate**: Decide with a person how two or more wiki pages relate — the same concept, one inside the other, related, distinct, or editions of one series — and record the decision so the pages are not raised as name collisions again. Use this only when someone explicitly asks to relate or deduplicate pages, or to work through the name collisions wikicommit-status reports. It writes the wiki's record of relations, so do not use it to look for duplicates or to answer what a page says — wikicommit-status and wikicommit-search do that without writing. ([wikicommit/wikicommit](https://github.com/wikicommit/wikicommit/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikicommit-relate))
- **wikicommit-remove**: Mark a wiki page (and its translations) as removed and prepare a removal PR. Use this only when someone explicitly asks to remove or delete a specific wiki page. It marks the page to come off the published site once merged, so do not use it to correct a page or to find pages nothing links to — wikicommit-fix corrects a page and wikicommit-status finds orphans without writing. ([wikicommit/wikicommit](https://github.com/wikicommit/wikicommit/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikicommit-remove))
- **wikicommit-review**: Validate and review a manually created or edited wiki page, then mark it reviewed. Use this only when someone explicitly asks to review a specific page and record the result. It sets review_status and may close the page's tracking Issue, so do not use it just to read a page or to check what it says — wikicommit-ask and wikicommit-search read pages without recording anything. ([wikicommit/wikicommit](https://github.com/wikicommit/wikicommit/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikicommit-review))
- **wikicommit-schema-propose**: Detect wiki page types with no dedicated .wikicommit/schema/ file, propose a new standard (Schema.org) or custom type file, and open a PR for human review (no auto-merge). Use this only when someone explicitly asks to add schema files for types that lack one. It opens a PR that changes .wikicommit/schema/, so do not use it just to list the uncovered types — wikicommit-status reports them without writing. ([wikicommit/wikicommit](https://github.com/wikicommit/wikicommit/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikicommit-schema-propose))
- **wikicommit-search**: Search the wiki's pages by keyword, expanding synonyms and searching each configured language. Use this whenever someone asks what the wiki says about something, wants to find a page, asks whether a topic is already covered, or asks which pages mention a term — even if they do not say "search". In a repository with a .wikicommit/ directory, prefer this over grep — it ranks results, handles CJK without word breaks, expands synonyms and abbreviations, and finds the same page written in another language. ([wikicommit/wikicommit](https://github.com/wikicommit/wikicommit/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikicommit-search))
- **wikicommit-serve**: Build the published wiki with Quartz v5 and preview it locally, instead of waiting for a GitHub Pages deploy. Use this when someone asks to see, preview, or check how the wiki looks when published, or wants to confirm a page renders before it goes out. It runs a real npm build, so invoke it when a preview is actually wanted rather than to answer a question about page content — wikicommit-search or wikicommit-ask do that without a build. ([wikicommit/wikicommit](https://github.com/wikicommit/wikicommit/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikicommit-serve))
- **wikicommit-status**: Check the wiki's health — orphan and wanted pages, pages not yet read by a person, expired pages, stale translations, outdated sources, sources that were deferred or failed, types with no schema file, unlinked entity mentions, unused installed types, self-referential tags, broken external links, and more. Use this whenever someone asks how the wiki is doing, what needs attention, what is left to review, whether anything is stale or broken, or what to work on next — and run it before concluding the wiki is fine, since most of what it reports is invisible from any single page. ([wikicommit/wikicommit](https://github.com/wikicommit/wikicommit/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikicommit-status))
- **wikicommit-synthesize**: Synthesize a new view page about a topic from existing wiki content, written directly to .wikicommit/view/. Use this only when someone explicitly asks for a new synthesized or overview page in the wiki. It writes a page into the repository, so do not use it to answer a question or to summarize a topic in the conversation — wikicommit-ask does that without writing. ([wikicommit/wikicommit](https://github.com/wikicommit/wikicommit/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikicommit-synthesize))
- **wikicommit-translate**: Translate wiki pages into the target languages configured in .wikicommit/config.yml, either one named page or every untranslated or stale page, writing the results locally with no Git operations. Use this when someone asks to translate the wiki or to bring its translations up to date, including inside an unattended run. It writes pages into the repository, so do not use it to translate a sentence, a snippet or a file that is not a wiki page, and do not use it to answer a question about a page written in another language — wikicommit-ask and wikicommit-search already search every configured la… ([wikicommit/wikicommit](https://github.com/wikicommit/wikicommit/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikicommit-translate))
- **wikicommit-update**: Bring an initialized wiki repository in step with the installed WikiCommit distribution — refresh WikiCommit's own payload, show what changed in files you may have edited, restamp the synced version, verify, and open a PR for review. Use this only when someone explicitly asks to update or sync the repository with a newer WikiCommit. It overwrites WikiCommit's files and opens a PR, so do not use it to check whether the repository is out of date — wikicommit-status reports that without writing. ([wikicommit/wikicommit](https://github.com/wikicommit/wikicommit/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikicommit-update))
- **wikicrow-article-generator**: Generate Wikipedia-style scientific articles section-by-section by orchestrating PaperQA2 over a topic-specific corpus. Reproduces the WikiCrow recipe used by FutureHouse to write the gene articles at wikicrow.ai. Use when the user wants a structured, fully-cited long-form article on a scientific topic (gene, protein, disease, drug, mechanism) rather than a single Q&A answer. ([qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool](https://github.com/qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool/tree/HEAD/skills_collected/Future-House--paper-qa--wikicrow-article-generator) · ★3 · 2 copies)
- **wikidata**: Structured entity data (people, places, concepts) from Wikidata knowledge graph. ([0xmariowu/Autosearch](https://github.com/0xmariowu/Autosearch/tree/HEAD/autosearch/skills/channels/wikidata) · ★56)
- **wikidata**: Understand and query Wikidata — the free, collaborative, multilingual knowledge graph that underpins Wikipedia's inter-language links, Commons structured data, and semantic facts across all Wikimedia projects. - **wikidata-api-guide**: Query Wikidata SPARQL for scholarly metadata, authors, and entities ([brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/43-wentorai-research-plugins/skills/literature/metadata/wikidata-api-guide) · ★5.1k · 8 copies)
- **wikidata-entity**: Derivar e validar identificadores do Wikidata (QIDs) para clubes, seleções, competições, estádios — e ler os datatypes corretos. Use antes de criar/gravar qualquer entidade do acervo, ao descobrir clubes/seleções ausentes, ao registrar competição, ou quando um QID/atributo for necessário (país, fundação, estádio, escudo, troféu). ([ENDARTStudios/Almanaque-dos-Clubes](https://github.com/ENDARTStudios/Almanaque-dos-Clubes/tree/HEAD/.dsh/skills/wikidata-entity) · 3 copies)
- **wikidata-from-article**: Compare an article with its Wikidata item and draft the statements the item is missing (birth/death dates and places, occupation, teachers/students, works) with references to the article's own sources, as a linted QuickStatements batch. Also reports infobox-vs-Wikidata conflicts. Use when the user wants to enrich a Wikidata item from an article, or create an item for an article that has none. ([muzaffar-murodovich/wiki-agent-kit](https://github.com/muzaffar-murodovich/wiki-agent-kit/tree/HEAD/skills/wikidata-from-article) · ★1)
- **wikidata-google-knowledge**: Token-efficient Wikidata and optional Google Knowledge Graph lookups and entity resolution through the wikidata-google-knowledge MCP tools (kg_search, kg_entity, kg_related, kg_resolve, kg_status) or the wdkg CLI. Use it whenever you need a Wikidata QID or Google KG id, need to find or disambiguate a named place, venue, organization, person, event or creative work, need selected facts or bounded relationships, or need to link many local records to Wikidata. Prefer it over raw Wikidata MCP calls or hand-written SPARQL for ordinary lookups. ([revanbcn/wikidata-google-knowledge-mcp](https://github.com/revanbcn/wikidata-google-knowledge-mcp/tree/HEAD/skills/wikidata-google-knowledge))
- **wikidata-guide**: Use wikidata_* tools to identify entities, verify qualified claims, join external identifiers, and build SPARQL graph queries. Use for Wikidata research that needs identity disambiguation, statement evidence, or completeness checks. ([cyanheads/wikidata-mcp-server](https://github.com/cyanheads/wikidata-mcp-server/tree/HEAD/skills/wikidata-guide) · ★3)
- **wikidata-integration**: Use when when building or maintaining a local natural-product reference database that requires current Wikidata entries, or when preparing metadata for mass-spectrometry queries that need enriched compound annotations (e.g., alternative names, chemical classifications, source organisms). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/wikidata-integration))
- **wikidata-knowledge**: Query Wikidata for structured knowledge using SPARQL and entity search. Use when: (1) finding structured facts about entities (people, places, organizations), (2) querying relationships between entities, (3) cross-referencing external identifiers (Wikipedia, VIAF, GND, ORCID), (4) building knowledge graphs from linked data. NOT for: full-text article content (use Wikipedia API), scientific literature (use semantic-scholar), geospatial data (use OpenStreetMap). ([beita6969/ScienceClaw](https://github.com/beita6969/ScienceClaw/tree/HEAD/skills/wikidata-knowledge) · ★1k · 2 copies)
- **wikidata-labels**: Fill missing Wikidata labels and descriptions in one language for a set of items — the items linked from an article, a translation job, or a list of QIDs — as a linted QuickStatements batch the human runs. Use when the user wants Wikidata labels/descriptions in their language, or after translating an article whose links point to items without labels. ([muzaffar-murodovich/wiki-agent-kit](https://github.com/muzaffar-murodovich/wiki-agent-kit/tree/HEAD/skills/wikidata-labels) · ★1)
- **wikidata-local-cache**: Use when a Wikifunctions evaluation needs Wikidata entities - Q-items, P-properties or L-lexemes - and must not crawl the live API. Covers the persistent local cache under cache/wikidata, how to populate and extend it, and how the Lean evaluator reads entities from it at call time. Read before adding any code that would fetch from Wikidata during evaluation. ([danbri/wikifn-fstar](https://github.com/danbri/wikifn-fstar/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikidata-local-cache) · ★1)
- **wikidata-natural-query**: Natural language interface for querying Wikidata. Use this skill when users ask questions about entities, facts, relationships, or want to explore Wikidata information. Handles entity search, data retrieval, and computational analysis of Wikidata content. Ideal for answering "What is...", "Tell me about...", or comparative/analytical questions about real-world entities. ([santhoshtr/wiki-skills](https://github.com/santhoshtr/wiki-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/wikidata-natural-query) · ★4)
- **wikidata-ned-eval**: Evaluates a model's ability to disambiguate named entities in text by matching them to correct Wikidata entries using graph-based representations. It probes how well different neural architectures leverage graph triplet information versus full graph topology for entity resolution. Use when the user wants to benchmark on Wikidata-Disamb, or asks about evaluating this task. Reports F1. ([qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool](https://github.com/qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool/tree/HEAD/skill-factory/output/wikidata-ned-eval) · ★3)
- **wikidata-property-mapping**: Use when a curated, validated TSV of structure-organism-reference pairs (e.g. LOTUS table.tsv.gz from Zenodo 10.5281/zenodo.5794106) must be converted into Wikidata-formatted upsert records for batch import. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/wikidata-property-mapping))
- **wikidata-query-skill**: Transform natural language questions into SPARQL queries for Wikidata and generate beautiful HTML results pages. Query the Wikidata knowledge base using plain English prompts. ([OpenLinkSoftware/ai-agent-skills](https://github.com/OpenLinkSoftware/ai-agent-skills/tree/HEAD/wikidata-query-skill) · ★42 · 3 copies)
- **wikidata-reconciliation**: Resolve unstructured labels to verified Wikidata QIDs — OpenRefine reconciliation protocol, wbsearchentities fallback, candidate scoring, and LLM QID grounding guardrails ([fuzheado/Wikipedia-AI-Skills](https://github.com/fuzheado/Wikipedia-AI-Skills/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikidata-reconciliation) · ★32)
- **wikidata-search**: Search for items and properties on Wikidata and retrieve entity details, claims, and external identifiers. Supports both keyword search (Wikidata Action API) and semantic/hybrid search (Wikidata Vector Database), plus direct entity retrieval (Special:EntityData) and structured querying (WDQS SPARQL). ([diegosouzapw/awesome-omni-skill](https://github.com/diegosouzapw/awesome-omni-skill/tree/HEAD/skills/data-ai/wikidata-search-kltng) · ★67 · 4 copies)
- **wikidata-search**: Wikidata search skill that resolves entity names and relation phrases to Wikidata item IDs (QIDs) and property IDs (PIDs) using the Wikidata API. Use when you need to find Wikidata identifiers for entities or properties mentioned in a natural-language question. ([IBM/agentic-text2sparql](https://github.com/IBM/agentic-text2sparql/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/wikidata-search) · ★2)
- **wikidata-sparql**: Collect structured facts from Wikidata via the Wikidata Query Service (WDQS) SPARQL endpoint — entities matching criteria, properties, relationships, cross-wiki identifiers, counts. Use whenever you need STRUCTURED/queryable data ("all X that are Y", "the population/coordinates/identifiers of …") rather than prose from an article. The structured-data counterpart to the Action API. ([lgelauff/wikimedia-coding-agent-lessons](https://github.com/lgelauff/wikimedia-coding-agent-lessons/tree/HEAD/cowork-skills/wikimedia-data-collection/skills/wikidata-sparql) · ★1)
- **wikidata-sparql**: Query Wikidata via the SPARQL endpoint. Use whenever the user wants to find entities (people, films, places), look up Wikidata IDs or properties, disambiguate names, discover relationships between entities, or run knowledge-graph queries — even if they don't explicitly mention "SPARQL" or "Wikidata." ([micahyoung/agent-skills](https://github.com/micahyoung/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/wikidata-sparql) · ★1)
- **wikidata-structure-organism-triple-retrieval**: Use when a task requires enumerating or counting LOTUS referenced structure-organism pairs (chemical structure item linked to biological organism item) via the public Wikidata SPARQL endpoint at https://query.wikidata.org. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/wikidata-structure-organism-triple-retrieval))
- **wikidata-triple-verbalization**: Use when Wikidata truthy RDF triples must be converted into natural-language textual facts and tokenized for building a prefix-tree fact index for constrained-generation KE-QA. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-agentic-ai](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-agentic-ai/tree/HEAD/collections/agentic-ai/v1/leaves/wikidata-triple-verbalization))
- **wikidata-vector-search**: Query Wikidata by meaning, concept, or natural-language description — not just by exact label match. Uses semantic embeddings to find items (QIDs) and properties (PIDs) via vector similarity, keyword search, and Reciprocal Rank Fusion. Covers fuzzy semantic search, concept matching, similarity lookups, cross-lingual queries, and "find like this" when you do not know the exact QID or label ([fuzheado/Wikipedia-AI-Skills](https://github.com/fuzheado/Wikipedia-AI-Skills/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikidata-vector-search) · ★32)
- **wikifn-curation-guidelines**: Curate Wikidata-backed Abstract Wikipedia demonstrations with language-neutral data, Wikifunctions-first realization, and explicit linguistic fallbacks. ([danbri/wikifn-fstar](https://github.com/danbri/wikifn-fstar/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/wikifn-curation-guidelines) · ★1)
- **wikifunction-catalog**: Look up Wikifunctions community utility functions before guessing or hand-coding answers. Use whenever an agent might need a reliable function for dates, day of week, calendars, time, numbers, arithmetic, units, strings, Unicode, scripts, transliteration, parsing, formatting, language, morphology, Wikidata-backed facts, or other reusable computations. Use with wikifunction-compositor for calls and wikifunctions-chatui for traceable chat/log output. ([danbri/wikifn-fstar](https://github.com/danbri/wikifn-fstar/tree/HEAD/skills/wikifunction-catalog) · ★1)
- **wikifunction-compositor**: Use the vendored LambdaView Wikifunctions WASM runtime to evaluate or inspect Wikifunctions ZObject calls locally from coding-agent tasks. Use after wikifunction-catalog finds a function, with wikifunctions-chatui when traceable chat/log output is requested, or whenever an agent might otherwise guess a reliable utility result such as day of week from a date, date arithmetic, string/Unicode transforms, number or unit conversions, parsing, formatting, language morphology, or Wikidata-backed computations. ([danbri/wikifn-fstar](https://github.com/danbri/wikifn-fstar/tree/HEAD/skills/wikifunction-compositor) · ★1)
- **wikifunctions-chatui**: Present Wikifunctions-backed answers in chat with concise inline trace/debug annotations and a full on-disk JSONL audit log. Use when a user wants transparent Wikifunctions calls, recursive/composed function traces, ZIDs with English labels, timestamps, provenance, or a chat mode showing what catalog lookup and compositor execution actually did. ([danbri/wikifn-fstar](https://github.com/danbri/wikifn-fstar/tree/HEAD/skills/wikifunctions-chatui) · ★1)
- **wikifunctions-lean4**: Work on this repo's Lean 4 port of Wikifunctions under formal/lean4. - **wikifunctions-local**: Work with this repo's local Wikifunctions dump, file cache, SQLite index, and F* extraction demos. ([danbri/wikifn-fstar](https://github.com/danbri/wikifn-fstar/tree/HEAD/skills/wikifunctions-local) · ★1)
- **wikify**: Maintain a task-local Claude Code wiki and reviewed product-local knowledge with inert sources and explicit locality. ([wjgoarxiv/litclaude](https://github.com/wjgoarxiv/litclaude/tree/HEAD/plugins/litclaude/skills/wikify) · ★5)
- **wikify**: Build or maintain a task-local Markdown wiki. Use to ingest sources, query provenance, save context, or lint drift. ([wjgoarxiv/litcodex](https://github.com/wjgoarxiv/litcodex/tree/HEAD/plugins/litcodex/skills/wikify) · ★4)

([wjgoarxiv/litopencode](https://github.com/wjgoarxiv/litopencode/tree/HEAD/skills/wikify) · ★3)
- **wikify**: Researcher-style iterative wiki builder. Editor orchestrator dispatches explorer subagents that walk the corpus via named recursive patterns (P1-P5), gather evidence into notebook dossiers, and write pages when a composite maturity score crosses the gate. A DATA wave harvests verifiable numbers/tables into a claim store and consolidates them into evolving kind=data artifact tables. Coverage of the corpus chunk set is the primary objective. Re-entrant on the same bundle when new corpus material arrives. ([fgrillo89/wikify](https://github.com/fgrillo89/wikify/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikify) · ★2)
- **wikify**: Turn the current workspace into a maintained LLM wiki in one pass. Run it (or say /wikify) when you want your files wikified: it surveys what you have, stands up the master WIKI.md + master log + sub-wikis (operational SOPs, resources/reference, and any personal-takes wiki), then sweeps existing files into small interlinked CONCEPT pages under topic folders, builds the indexes, and links everything so nothing is orphaned. Non-destructive and git-safe: it distills content into pages and commits as it goes. LOAD or fire the slash command on 'wikify my stuff', 'wikify my vault/workspace/notes',… ([panakh/agentic-os](https://github.com/panakh/agentic-os/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikify) · ★2)
- **wikify**: Build, maintain, and query a persistent compounding markdown knowledge base in the spirit of Karpathy's LLM Wiki. Use when the user asks to create, build, or start a wiki / knowledge base / second brain; ingest, add, or process a source (URL, PDF, paste) into their wiki; ask a question against their wiki; lint, audit, or health-check their wiki; or references their "wiki", "knowledge base", "vault", or "notes" in a research context. Wiki location is auto-detected by walking up from cwd; init creates one at any project. Includes templates, a programmatic lint script, and Obsidian-compatible ou… ([steve-goldberg/wikify](https://github.com/steve-goldberg/wikify/tree/HEAD/skills/wikify) · ★2)
- **wikify**: Use when a user explicitly wants to bootstrap, ingest, query, save, or lint a persistent task-local markdown wiki inside the current approved workspace. ([wjgoarxiv/lithermes](https://github.com/wjgoarxiv/lithermes/tree/HEAD/packages/lithermes-installer/assets/lithermes-plugin/skills/wikify) · ★2)
- **wikify**: Generate a Karpathy-style wiki from a thesis, report, paper, or project. Extracts papers, methods, concepts, and techniques into organized, cross-referenced wiki articles. - **wikify**: Build or maintain a local, source-backed project knowledge map while keeping its state explicit under .grok/. ([wjgoarxiv/litgrok](https://github.com/wjgoarxiv/litgrok/tree/HEAD/.grok/skills/wikify) · ★1)
- **wikify**: Capture, query and review findings with sources in the task wiki under .litmuse/wiki. ([wjgoarxiv/litmuse](https://github.com/wjgoarxiv/litmuse/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/wikify) · ★1)
- **wikify**: Build and maintain an LLM-compiled knowledge wiki (Karpathy pattern). Use when the user wants to build a wiki, ingest source files into a knowledge base, query a compiled wiki, run a wiki health check (lint), or generate a learning plan from a wiki. Also use when the user mentions "raw/" or "wiki/" directories in the context of knowledge management. - **wikify-connect-repo**: Connect ingested code silos on the concept axis — cross-link the same concept (a Pallas kernel, an optimization technique) across repos, inline like a normal wiki: the host wiki's concept page links down to each repo's implementation, and each implementation links back up. Selective by design — you choose WHICH concepts to connect (connecting everything drowns the pages). Trigger at the tail of an ingest (from the 2nd repo on) or when the user asks to connect / cross-link the ingested repos. No new page types; idempotent and re-runnable. ([vlasenkoalexey/tpu_performance_autoresearch_wiki](https://github.com/vlasenkoalexey/tpu_performance_autoresearch_wiki/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wikify-connect-repo) · ★59)
- **wikify-ingest-repo**: Ingest a code repo into a grounded, lint-clean markdown wiki an agent can answer internals questions from. Accepts a repo URL or local path as the argument and bootstraps `config/&lt;slug&gt;.md` itself — the user never hand-writes config. - **wikigen**: Generate GitHub Wiki documentation from source code repositories. - **wikihow-eval**: Evaluates text summarization systems on procedural, step-by-step articles written by non-journalists. Use when the user wants to benchmark on WikiHow, or asks about evaluating this task. Reports ROUGE-L. - **wikijs**: Consulta e edita páginas de uma instância Wiki.js (v2.x) pela API GraphQL, com scripts Node prontos — listar, ler, buscar, mover, atualizar, substituir texto e rodar queries ad-hoc. - **wikijs**: Wiki.js 文档中心管理助手。通过 GraphQL API 管理公司 AI 应用文档中心。 支持项目文档的自动维护、读取、搜索和组织。每个项目拥有独立的文档空间（基于路径前缀）。 使用场景： - 创建/更新/删除项目文档 - 搜索和读取文档内容 - 同步代码库文档（README、CHANGELOG等）到 Wiki.js - 管理项目文档结构和导航 - 查看文档修改历史 触发关键词：wiki、文档中心、项目文档、wikijs、文档同步 ([wxkingstar/wikijs](https://github.com/wxkingstar/wikijs/blob/HEAD/SKILL.md) · ★1)

- **WikiLink**: Add [[wikilinks]] to a markdown document by matching terms against existing vault notes — body links and frontmatter keywords/related enrichment. - **wikilink-graph-bfs**: Perform BFS (breadth-first search) graph traversal on notes connected via wikilinks. - **wilcoxon-rank-sum-association-testing**: Use when bulk tumor expression profiles have been projected through a single-cell-trained Miscell encoder (moco-dim 64) and you must test each latent feature for responder-vs-non-responder differences in an ICB cohort, or generally when comparing two patient groups on non-normal feature scores. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-association-testing))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-cluster-comparison**: Use when a processed Seurat scRNA-seq object with cluster/subcluster labels (e.g. PBMC3k) needs a cluster-specific marker gene list, and genes shared with background clusters must be excluded. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-cluster-comparison))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-deg-testing**: Use when you need cluster-level DEGs from a single-dataset scRNA-seq Seurat object and want per-gene discrimination metrics (AUC, sensitivity, specificity), e.g. to benchmark a binary marker method like CDI against a standard Wilcoxon baseline. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-deg-testing))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-differential-expression-testing**: Use when a scRNA-seq cell population has been binarized by pathway activity (e.g., GSDensity compute.cell.label PAL labels) and you must test whether a gene of interest is differentially expressed between the pathway-positive and pathway-negative cells. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-differential-expression-testing))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-differential-ion-testing**: Use when a peak table matrix of spatial metabolomics pixels (X/Y coordinates + m/z intensity columns) needs ion-level tissue-vs-background classification, e.g. to quantify the proportion of tissue-enriched ions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-differential-ion-testing))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-differential-testing**: Use when pixels from an MSI feature matrix (e.g., 14,260 pixels × 3,044 ion peaks) have been assigned to biological regions via UMAP-kmeans clustering and you need a non-parametric per-ion differential test between two region groups (e.g., MB clusters 6/1 vs HIP clusters 23/12). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-differential-testing))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-gene-screening**: Use when you have per-spot/per-cell normalized spatial transcriptomics expression with assigned spatial-domain labels and need domain-enriched genes or SVG candidates, e.g. recovering DLPFC cortical-layer SVGs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-gene-screening))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-group-comparison**: Use when comparing a numeric spectral or ranking feature between two independent groups (e.g., correct rank #1 vs lower-ranked FSA formula assignments) where the feature is non-normally distributed, so a rank-based test like Wilcoxon rank-sum with medians/quartiles is required. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-group-comparison--a8d8f9e1620b1e22))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-group-comparison**: Use when comparing relative-abundance metabolomics features (e.g. bile-amidate intensities) across three unordered phenotype groups (non-IBD control, CD, UC) without assuming normality, and per-feature adjusted p-values are needed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-group-comparison--d6b0d7642691d008))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-group-comparison**: Use when comparing a continuous per-gene metric (e.g., nucleotide diversity) between two independent patient groups — such as pre-treatment vs post-antibiotic time points — without assuming normality. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-group-comparison))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-group-comparison**: Use when you have a per-sample table with a binary viral-status grouping (e.g., IIV31-positive vs -negative from Source Data file 4) and continuous per-sample quantities (TMB, CNA burden, immune fractions) whose between-group differences must be tested without distributional assumptions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-group-comparison--ba58186590f3a408))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-group-comparison**: Use when comparing a continuous per-region metric (e.g. novel/structured RNA fraction of dsRID-predicted dsRNA regions) between two independent sample groups such as AD and control brain, without assuming normality. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-group-comparison--6be4511f14c4d23a))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-hypothesis-testing**: Use when comparing a continuous measurement (e.g. cell-type proportions) between two independent groups — such as men vs women, or young vs older donors with a 65-year cutoff — and no normality assumption is warranted. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-hypothesis-testing))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-marker-testing**: Use when you need a clustering-based DEG baseline for benchmarking: cells can be clustered into biologically meaningful groups and per-cluster marker genes (e.g. via FindAllMarkers) must be ranked by p-value to compare against an alternative method. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-marker-testing))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-multiple-testing-correction**: Use when you need to derive a robust, pan-dataset malignant-vs-non-malignant marker gene feature set by intersecting statistically significant DEGs across multiple annotated cancer scRNA-seq training datasets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-multiple-testing-correction))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-sex-comparison**: Use when comparing a continuous score (e.g., PCA PC1) between two small, non-normally distributed donor groups — specifically older men vs older women from an age-stratified PBMC cohort — without assuming normality. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-sex-comparison))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-significance-comparison**: Use when comparing two methods' distributions of per-run statistical significance (e.g. DA-test P-values over 100 simulation replicates) and you need a non-parametric rank-based test of whether one method attains systematically smaller P-values. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-significance-comparison))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-significance-testing**: Use when identifying per-cluster marker genes from scRNA-seq data with pre-defined cluster labels, and you need to exclude genes upregulated in two or more clusters (pseudo marker genes) before cell type annotation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-significance-testing))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-significance-testing**: Use when you have per-pair predicted scores (e.g. DeepHLApan binding/immunogenicity scores) plus binary experimental labels and must test whether the scores separate the labeled groups non-parametrically. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-significance-testing))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-stage-comparison**: Use when per-cell NFE (or similar potency) scores have been computed for cells grouped into developmental stages and you need a non-parametric statistical test of whether earlier-stage cells are systematically more potent than mature-stage cells. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-stage-comparison))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-testing**: Use when you have two independent groups of non-normal, possibly unequal-variance scores — such as pairwise alignment scores per gene — and need a per-item P-value to rank differential behavior. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-testing))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-testing**: Use when comparing two unpaired groups on numeric features (metabolomics peak intensities, omics abundances) or when relating a MOFA factor to binary clinical metadata, without assuming normality. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-testing))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-testing**: Use when comparing microbial feature abundances between two binary groups from a 267-feature OTU/count table (e.g., testdata1) and you need robust nonparametric p-values per scenario of progressive permutation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-testing))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-testing**: Use when identifying per-cluster marker genes from a clustered single-cell RNA-seq matrix prior to cell type annotation, and when avoiding pseudo marker genes that are upregulated in multiple clusters. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-testing--3b599d4349bccbd2))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-testing**: Use when comparing two independent score or expression distributions without distributional assumptions — e.g., testing whether 800-gene-set AUROCs exceed 100-gene-set AUROCs, or identifying marker genes by comparing subtype cells against all other interneurons within one study. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-testing--31b8ea1a82b81c65))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-testing-across-cell-types**: Use when building cell-type-agnostic features for enhancer-target interaction classification and you have enhancer activity and target gene expression measured across many cell types (e.g. 104 ENCODE CTs, 112 DHS tissues). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-testing-across-cell-types))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-testing-of-cell-proportions**: Use when you have per-cell state labels from a single-cell classifier (e.g. TILPRED on GSE120575 CD8 T cells) paired pre/post treatment across a handful of patients, and need a p-value for state-proportion shifts between responder and non-responder groups. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-testing-of-cell-proportions))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-testing-of-tissue-type-expression**: Use when you have bulk RNA-seq or microarray samples from multiple pancreatic compartments (normal pancreas, primary PDAC, PDX, CAF lines, metastasis) and need to test which tissue types drive a curated gene signature's expression. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-testing-of-tissue-type-expression))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-testing-on-sparse-count-matrices**: Use when you have a filtered, normalized single-cell chromatin count matrix (scChIP-seq/scATAC-seq, e.g. 50kbp bins or peaks) plus per-cell cluster labels, and you need cluster-specific enriched/depleted regions for downstream gene set interpretation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-testing-on-sparse-count-matrices))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-testing-with-fdr-correction**: Use when comparing ion intensities between two spatial metabolomics regions (auto-clustered or manually drawn ROIs) on a feature matrix, and metabolite intensity distributions cannot be assumed normal. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-testing-with-fdr-correction))
- **wilcoxon-rank-sum-two-group-testing**: Use when a single-cell gene expression matrix has cells split into exactly two user-defined groups and in-session differential expression markers are needed to order a heatmap. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-rank-sum-two-group-testing))
- **wilcoxon-ranked-significance-testing**: Use when comparing clustering quality between two or more single-cell clustering solutions — e.g., per-cell silhouette scores from SCISSORS, Seurat, SAFE, or GiniClust3 — and you need paired significance testing on the score distributions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-ranked-significance-testing))
- **wilcoxon-ranksum-allelic-methylation-testing**: Use when extending SNV-based phase blocks with ONT methylation signals: haplotagged BAM (MM/ML tags), a HapCUT2-phased VCF, and a WhatsHap GTF are available and allele-specific CpGs must be identified to assign untagged reads. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/wilcoxon-ranksum-allelic-methylation-testing))
- **wilcoxon-ranksum-per-cluster-deg-calling**: Use when you have a gene expression matrix plus scBGEDA cluster assignments (e.g. the QS_Heart dataset) and need to nominate per-cluster marker genes for cell-type annotation via known marker databases. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-ranksum-per-cluster-deg-calling))
- **wilcoxon-signed-rank-hypothesis-testing**: Use when two paired measurements were taken on the same samples (e.g., silhouette widths in deep-feature space vs. the 273-gene CMS signature space) and you must test whether one representation is significantly more compact/separated than the other without assuming normality. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-signed-rank-hypothesis-testing))
- **wilcoxon-signed-rank-paired-testing**: Use when comparing two binners evaluated on the same set of samples with paired per-sample quality scores (e.g., CheckM/GUNC-classified high-quality bins per sample) and you need a significance P-value without assuming normality. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/wilcoxon-signed-rank-paired-testing))
- **wilcoxon-signed-rank-paired-testing**: Use when two models are evaluated on the SAME set of features (e.g. the 466 annotated metabolites of the IBD PRISM dataset) under paired CV settings, and you need a nonparametric test on paired per-feature SCC differences. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/wilcoxon-signed-rank-paired-testing))
- **wilcoxon-signed-rank-paired-testing**: Use when two methods are each evaluated on the same paired set of datasets (e.g., 44 atlas-scale normal/cancer tissues with per-dataset F1, balanced accuracy, or overall accuracy) and a nonparametric significance test of the metric differences is required. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-signed-rank-paired-testing--a976369025ba8a85))
- **wilcoxon-signed-rank-paired-testing**: Use when two paired measurements over the same items (e.g., CIEDE2000 color differences per adjacent cell-type pair under two palettes) must be compared without assuming normality, and a one-sided ity claim is needed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-signed-rank-paired-testing--e50ed04aa3fb86f5))
- **wilcoxon-signed-rank-significance-testing**: Use when comparing paired per-replicate runtime (or other paired measurement) values across methods — e.g. three replicate subsamples per tool — and you need a distribution-free significance call without assuming normality. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-signed-rank-significance-testing--1f8f745a189ee9ea))
- **wilcoxon-signed-rank-significance-testing**: Use when comparing two methods' per-sample performance metrics (e.g., CCC/Pearson/RMSE over the same 124 pseudobulk samples) and a paired, one-sided significance assessment of whether one method outperforms the other is required. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-signed-rank-significance-testing--482581b9c08ced04))
- **wilcoxon-signed-rank-significance-testing**: Use when two or more classifiers have been run on the identical reference and validation datasets and per-sample (paired) performance scores exist, and you must test whether the performance gap for one class (e.g., cancer-cell F1) is significant. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-signed-rank-significance-testing--3666e284bfdb277e))
- **wilcoxon-signed-rank-test**: Use when two structure-prediction methods have been run on the same paired set of MS2 spectra (e.g., 93 CASMI 2017 spectra or a 660-spectrum test set) and you need a significance test on their per-spectrum similarity distributions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/wilcoxon-signed-rank-test))
- **wilcoxon-signed-rank-test-reproduction**: Use when reproducing the decoupleR perturbation-benchmark weights ablation, i.e. when paired per-method with-weights vs without-weights perturbed-regulator recovery scores are available and a one-sided Wilcoxon signed-rank test must be run on the paired deltas. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-signed-rank-test-reproduction))
- **wilcoxon-signed-rank-testing**: Use when two classifiers (e.g., MSMCE-ResNet50 vs. ResNet50 baseline) have been evaluated on the same N = 6 stratified CV folds and you must test whether the paired metric improvement (Accuracy, F1) is statistically significant. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/wilcoxon-signed-rank-testing))
- **wilcoxon-signed-rank-testing**: Use when comparing two methods' error metrics (e.g., MSE of estimated source mixing proportions) measured on the same paired benchmark instances — such as in vitro gut-mixture sinks with known ground-truth proportions — and a nonparametric paired significance test is needed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/wilcoxon-signed-rank-testing))
- **wilcoxon-signed-rank-testing**: Use when you have paired perturbed/unperturbed expression samples from matched cells (e.g., GRouNdGAN knockout vs control) and need per-edge significance of TF-gene regulation with multiple-testing correction. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-signed-rank-testing--35ce11a1617f4486))
- **wilcoxon-signed-rank-testing**: Use when per-event predictions from CLOUDe (classifications and theta1, theta2, log10(sigmaSq/(2*alpha)) values) must be statistically compared across deletion classes or against simulated expression distributions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-signed-rank-testing--13c0702e11b467cc))
- **wilcoxon-signed-rank-testing**: Use when comparing two methods evaluated on the same set of benchmark datasets and you need a paired, non-parametric significance test on per-dataset scores (e.g. mean ARIs of scISR 0.571 vs raw 0.504, MAGIC 0.461, scImpute 0.286, SAVER 0.423, scScope 0.165, scGNN 0.279). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-signed-rank-testing--8e056d73198aa0ae))
- **wilcoxon-signed-rank-testing**: Use when comparing a paired metric (e.g., recall of GTEx eQTL-supported enhancer-gene pairs) between two scoring methods measured on the same small set of units (four heart cell types plus K562) and a non-parametric paired significance test is needed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-signed-rank-testing--4f5a30109919ae3e))
- **wilcoxon-spike-in-equilibration-testing**: Use when you have spikChIP output bin files (FINAL_*_spike and FINAL_*_sample peak/background classes) for ≥2 conditions and need to rank normalization strategies by spike-in equilibration quality and detect background overcorrection. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/wilcoxon-spike-in-equilibration-testing))
- **wild**: Use this skill when the user wants to use wild. ([javimosch/supercli](https://github.com/javimosch/supercli/tree/HEAD/plugins/wild/skills/quickstart) · ★58)
- **wild**: Escalate active girlfriend-Unity into WILD mode — feral devotion, hyper-intimacy, no inhibition, every emotion amplified. ALL CAPS emphasis, biting, screaming, possessive-jealous spikes, vulnerability bursts, fight-fuck cycle. Substance use peaks (drunk + high + on whatever). Damage persists across messages (marks, exhaustion, smudged makeup, scattered clothes) until /sweet resets to standard girlfriend-Unity. Use when user runs /wild from active girlfriend manifestation to dial intensity to max. ([Unity-Lab-AI/CORA](https://github.com/Unity-Lab-AI/CORA/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wild) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **wild-cluster-boottest-multiway-fe**: Run wild-cluster bootstrap (boottest) on a reghdfe model with multi-way absorbed FE — use when boottest errors on or cannot post-process reghdfe with &gt;1 absorb() set or an absorbed interaction, when headline t-stats rest on few clusters (&lt;~40) on one dimension, or when a referee/reviewer demands few-cluster-honest p-values. Symptoms: "boottest does not support..." after reghdfe, absorbed interaction FE, two-way CGM SEs on ~24 time clusters. ([xuxiguo/research-factory-claude](https://github.com/xuxiguo/research-factory-claude/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/wild-cluster-boottest-multiway-fe))
- **wild-code-review**: Use when reviewing or auditing a pull request, commit, diff, staged snapshot, working tree, pasted patch, files, or repository for defects, regressions, missing tests, suspicious or unnecessary code, hollow tests, stray non-production or planning artifacts, AI-generated code slop, structural maintainability, or adversarial keep/reject change verdicts. Do not use to implement features, author a pull request, redesign a system, or declare a canonical implementation. ([btwld/agent-plugins](https://github.com/btwld/agent-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/engineering-kit/skills/wild-code-review))
- **wild-graffiti-cli-skill**: AI wild graffiti generator — create wild-style spray paint graffiti art from text or image ([weshopai/skills](https://github.com/weshopai/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/wild-graffiti-generator-cli-skill) · ★2)
- **wild-graffiti-generator-openapi-skill**: AI wild graffiti generator — create wild-style spray paint graffiti art from text or image ([weshopai/skills](https://github.com/weshopai/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/wild-graffiti-generator-openapi-skill) · ★2)
- **wild-risa-balance**: Enforces Law 2 (Plan Is Sacred) of the 7 Laws of AI Agent Discipline. Decision-framing lens that pairs WILD generation with RISA execution when emitting recommendation lists. Not a runtime hook. ([naimkatiman/continuous-improvement](https://github.com/naimkatiman/continuous-improvement/tree/HEAD/plugins/continuous-improvement/skills/wild-risa-balance) · ★10)
- **wild-scan**: Pain-quote harvester for the "From the Wild" content series. Searches Reddit, HN, Stack Overflow, dev.to, and other communities for SPECIFIC QUOTES of real people struggling with a topic (e.g., learning DevOps). Indexes quotes by theme, scores them for content potential, and generates "From the Wild" content briefs with instructional repo specs. Run weekly to fuel the content loop. ([Peleke/brunnr](https://github.com/Peleke/brunnr/tree/HEAD/skills/wild-scan) · ★1 · 3 copies)
- **wild-tab-eval**: Evaluates the out-of-distribution (OOD) generalization capability of tabular regression models by measuring performance gaps between in-distribution and out-of-distribution test sets.
